--- Commit Message ---
LU-19921 ptlrpc: add GSSIAM security context client

The GSSIAM Security Flavor is a new, lightweight sptlrpc
authentication mechanism (selected by admins via the gssiam
flavor name, e.g. srpc.flavor.*=gssiam or -o sptlrpc=gssiam)
designed to integrate Lustre with external enterprise Identity
and Access Management (IAM) systems.

This patch implements the client-side GSSIAM security
policy and context operations (gssiam_sec_cops in
lustre/ptlrpc/gss/sec_gssiam.c):

- Hybrid Protocol Architecture: Uses GSS framing and a
  SEC_CTX_INIT handshake during initial connection setup
  to authenticate identity, negotiate security handles, and
  install the IAM principal and mount subdirectory onto the
  server export. Subsequent Data and Metadata RPCs use
  lightweight Null framing (sec_null) for high-performance I/O
  without GSS wrapping overhead.

- Context & Session Management: Implements context creation,
  destruction, and lookups (gssiam_create_sec, gssiam_destroy_sec,
  gssiam_lookup_ctx). Uses RCU-protected pointers
  (rcu_dereference_protected / synchronize_rcu) to prevent
  races during concurrent context lookups and teardowns.

- Initialization Handshake: Implements gssiam_send_init_rpc()
  and gssiam_init_pack_request() to transmit IAM principal
  identities, mount subdirectories (lgmi_subdir), and upcall
  tokens to the target server during context establishment.

- RPC Buffer Handling: Implements request and reply buffer
  allocators (gssiam_alloc_reqbuf, gssiam_free_reqbuf,
  gssiam_alloc_repbuf, gssiam_free_repbuf) that handle GSS
  headers for init RPCs while delegating standard traffic to
  null_accept_common().

- Lifetime & Expiration: Integrates with Lustre upcall_cache
  (upcall_cache_invalidate_one) to manage context caching,
  expiration, and invalidation across evictions and remounts.
  Initializes gssiam_upcall_cache in sptlrpc_gssiam_init() using
  the default userspace upcall helper /usr/sbin/l_gssiam_upcall
  (GSSIAM_UPCALL_PATH in sec_gssiam.c).

- Framing & Early Reply Helpers:
  * do_cli_unwrap_reply(): checks rq_repmsg != rq_repdata
    to distinguish wrapped vs unwrapped replies across all
    flavors, ensuring unwrapped replies retain their swab
    mask.
  * sptlrpc_cli_unwrap_early_reply(): propagates rq_ctx_init
    and rq_gss_framed to early_req to allow early replies
    for init and GSS-framed requests to be unwrapped.
  * ptlrpc_server_handle_req_in() & sptlrpc_svc_unwrap_request():
    add SPTLRPC_POLICY_GSSIAM arms for server-side framing
    support.
  * gss_exit_keyring(): drops __exit annotation as it is
    called by sptlrpc_gss_exit.

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-07-28 11:24:04.000000000) ---
Patch Set 39:

(17 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. The policy is deliberately not registered yet ("XXX register GSSIAM policy once all patches are landed"), so none of the new client/server callbacks execute today - but several of them will fault or LBUG the moment sptlrpc_register_policy() is added, so they are worth fixing in this patch rather than in the one that turns the flavor on. Two of them are corroborated by dead exports added here: null_alloc_repbuf()/null_free_repbuf() and null_accept_common() are made non-static and exported, yet nothing calls them, which lines up exactly with the two gaps in gssiam_sec_cops/gssiam_accept. No test coverage is added either; a sanity-sec case that mounts with the gssiam flavor would catch most of this once the policy is live.

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-03 08:40:30.000000000) ---
Patch Set 44:

(14 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. The two that matter most are the NULL upcall_cache_ops handed to upcall_cache_init(), and the way gssiam_accept() decides whether an incoming RPC is GSS-framed or Null-framed. Nothing here is live yet because sptlrpc_gssiam_init() does not register the policy, so both are latent until the rest of the series lands - but the client path is fully written in this patch, so it would be good to settle them now rather than in a later patch. There is also no test coverage for the new flavor; a sanity-sec case that mounts with 'gssiam' and exercises SEC_CTX_INIT would be valuable once the policy is registered.

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-24 11:17:38.000000000) ---
Patch Set 68:

(2 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. Worth flagging up front for anyone reading this in isolation: sptlrpc_gssiam_init() deliberately stops short of sptlrpc_register_policy(), so policies[SPTLRPC_POLICY_GSSIAM] stays NULL and sptlrpc_wireflavor2policy() has no request_module() fallback for anything but SPTLRPC_POLICY_GSS. Every client path added in sec_gssiam.c is therefore dead until a later patch lands, while the sptlrpc_name2flavor_base() hunk makes `gssiam` an accepted name right now - so a `srpc.flavor.*=gssiam` rule parses and stores fine and then fails at connect time with only "invalid flavor 0x3". Staging it this way is a fair call, but the half that is admin-visible landing ahead of the half that works is worth a note in the message.
